{"index":1,"delta":{"text":"---\n\n### 2.2 Multi-Factor Rough Bergomi (rBergomi) Model and Pathwise Properties\n\nThe **","type":"text"},"event_type":"step.delta"}

{"index":1,"delta":{"text":" exponential of a Riemann-Liouville fractional Brownian motion (also known as a Type-II fBm or Holmgren-R","type":"text"},"event_type":"step.delta"}
{"index":1,"delta":{"text":"iemann-Liouville process). We formulate here the generalized **Multi-Factor Rough Bergomi Model** with $K$ rough","type":"text"},"event_type":"step.delta"}
{"index":1,"delta":{"text":" factors having distinct Hurst exponents $H_1, \\dots, H_K \\in (0, 1/2)$","type":"text"},"event_type":"step.delta"}
{"index":1,"delta":{"text":" and mean-reversion/weight parameters.\n\nLet $W = (W^{(1)}, \\dots, W^{(K)}, W^\\","type":"text"},"event_type":"step.delta"}
{"index":1,"delta":{"text":"perp)^\\top$ be a $(K+1)$-dimensional standard Brownian motion on $(\\Omega, \\mathcal{F},","type":"text"},"event_type":"step.delta"}
{"index":1,"delta":{"text":" (\\mathcal{F}_t)_{t \\in [0,T]}, \\mathbb{Q})$. For each factor $k \\","type":"text"},"event_type":"step.delta"}
{"index":1,"delta":{"text":"in \\{1, \\dots, K\\}$, define the normalized Riemann-Liouville Volterra process:\n$$\\hat{W}_t","type":"text"},"event_type":"step.delta"}

\\frac{1}{2} \\gamma \\sigma^2(V) + \\frac{4 \\bar{\\lambda} \\kappa}{e} \\left( h_2(t, V) - \\frac{1}{2}\\epsilon \\right)^2 = 0, \\quad h_2(T, V) = -\\alpha_{\\text{term}},$$\n   which solves explicitly via hyperbolic tangent when $V$ is frozen at its local forward value $\\bar{V}$:\n   $$h_2(t, \\bar{V}) = \\frac{1}{2}\\epsilon - \\left( \\beta - \\frac{\\alpha_{++} - \\alpha_{-+}}{e} \\right) h_1(t) + \\frac{2}{e} \\left( h_2(t, \\bar{V}) - \\frac{1}{2}\\epsilon + \\frac{1}{2}(\\alpha_{++} - \\alpha_{-+}) g_1(t) \\right) = 0, \\quad h_1(T) = 0.$$\n3. **Resulting Analytical Optimal Quotes:**\n   $$\\delta_t^{\\pm, *} \\approx \\frac{1}{\\kappa} + (1 \\mp q) \\epsilon - (2 q \\mp 1) h_2(t, V_t) \\pm \\left( \\lambda_t^+ - \\lambda_t^- \\right) h_1(t) - \\left( \\alpha_{++} - \\alpha_{-+} \\right) q h_1(t).$$\n   Notice the transparent financial interpretation of every term:\n   * $\\frac{1}{\\kappa} + \\epsilon$: Base monopolistic markup plus adverse selection protection.\n   * $-(2q \\mp 1) h_2(t, V_t)$ (with $h_2 < 0$): **Rough-volatility-scaled inventory skew**; when the dealer is long ($q > 0$) and rough volatility $V_t$ spikes, $|h_2|$ increases, lowering both bid and ask quotes to aggressively offload inventory.\n   * $\\pm (\\lambda_t^+ - \\lambda_t^-) h_1(t)$: **Hawkes order-flow imbalance (OFI) alpha skew**; when buy intensity exceeds sell intensity ($\\lambda_t^+ > \\lambda_t^-$), the dealer skews quotes upward in anticipation of upward price pressure and self-exciting buy clusters.\n\n---\n\n## Part IV: Numerical Methods, Simulation Schemes, and Calibration Algorithms\n\n### 4.1 Multi-Exponential Markovian Approximation of Fractional Kernels\n\nTo simulate SVEs or solve the fractional Riccati-Volterra equation in $O(N)$ time instead of $O(N^2)$ time, we approximate the completely monotone Riemann-Liouville kernel $K_H(\\tau) = \\frac{\\tau^{H-1/2}}{\\Gamma(H+1/2)}$ by a finite sum of $M$ exponentials:\n$$K_H(\\tau) = \\int_0^\\infty e^{-\\gamma \\tau} \\, \\mu_H(d\\gamma) \\approx K_H^{(M)}(\\tau) := \\sum_{m=1}^M w_m e^{-\\gamma_m \\tau}, \\quad \\text{where } \\mu_H(d\\gamma) = \\frac{\\gamma^{-H - 1/2}}{\\Gamma(H + 1/2)\\Gamma(1/2 - H)} \\, d\\gamma.$$\n\n#### Proposition 4.1.1 (Geometric Partition Quadrature and $L^2([0,T])$ Error Bound)\nLet $[\\gamma_{\\min}, \\gamma_{\\max}]$ be a frequency range partitioned geometrically by $\\xi_m = \\xi_0 r^m$ for $m = 0, \\dots, M$ with geometric ratio $r = (\\gamma_{\\max} / \\gamma_{\\min})^{1/M} > 1$. On each sub-interval $[\\xi_{m-1}, \\xi_m]$, define the 0th- and 1st-moment-matched nodes and weights (Abi Jaber and El Euch, 2019):\n$$w_m = \\int_{\\xi_{m-1}}^{\\xi_m} \\mu_H(d\\gamma) = \\frac{\\xi_m^{1/2 - H} - \\xi_{m-1}^{1/2 - H}}{\\Gamma(H + 1/2)\\Gamma(3/2 - H)}, \\quad \\gamma_m = \\frac{1}{w_m} \\int_{\\xi_{m-1}}^{\\xi_m} \\gamma \\, \\mu_H(d\\gamma) = \\frac{1/2 - H}{3/2 - H} \\cdot \\frac{\\xi_m^{3/2 - H} - \\xi_{m-1}^{3/2 - H}}{\\xi_m^{1/2 - H} - \\xi_{m-1}^{1/2 - H}}.$$\nThen the $L^2([0,T])$ kernel approximation error satisfies:\n$$\\left\\| K_H - K_H^{(M)} \\right\\|_{L^2([0,T])} \\le C_1(H, T) \\xi_0^{1/2 - H} + C_2(H) \\xi_M^{-H} + C_3(H, T) (r - 1)^2.$$\nChoosing $\\xi_0 \\sim M^{-\\frac{2}{1/2-H}}$ and $\\xi_M \\sim M^{\\frac{2}{H}}$ yields a second-order convergence rate $\\|K_H - K_H^{(M)}\\|_{L^2([0,T])} = O\\left(M^{-2}\\ (\\ln M)^2\\right)$.\n\n##### Proof\nDecompose the error at $\\tau \\in (0,T]$ into three parts: the low-frequency tail $\\int_0^{\\xi_0} e^{-\\gamma \\tau} \\mu_H(d\\gamma) \\le \\int_0^{\\xi_0} \\mu_H(d\\gamma) = \\frac{\\xi_0^{1/2-H}}{\\Gamma(H+1/2)\\Gamma(3/2-H)}$, the high-frequency tail $\\int_{\\xi_M}^\\infty e^{-\\gamma \\tau} \\mu_H(d\\gamma)$, and the mid-range quadrature error $\\sum_{m=1}^M \\int_{\\xi_{m-1}}^{\\xi_m} (e^{-\\gamma \\tau} - e^{-\\gamma_m \\tau}) \\mu_H(d\\gamma)$.\nFor the high-frequency tail, integrating its square over $\\tau \\in [0,T]$ via Fubini's theorem:\n$$\\int_0^T \\left( \\int_{\\xi_M}^\\infty e^{-\\gamma \\tau} \\, \\mu_H(d\\gamma) \\right)^2 d\\tau \\le \\int_{\\xi_M}^\\infty \\int_{\\xi_M}^\\infty \\frac{1}{\\gamma_1 + \\gamma_2} \\, \\mu_H(d\\gamma_1) \\, \\mu_H(d\\gamma_2) = C_H \\xi_M^{-2H}.$$\nFor the mid-range quadrature error, since $w_m$ and $\\gamma_m$ match the 0th and 1st moments of $\\mu_H$ on $[\\xi_{m-1}, \\xi_m]$, a Taylor expansion of $\\gamma \\mapsto e^{-\\gamma \\tau}$ around $\\gamma_m$ cancels the linear term $(\\gamma - \\gamma_m)$, leaving $\\frac{1}{2} \\tau^2 e^{-\\tilde{\\gamma}_m \\tau} (\\gamma - \\gamma_m)^2 \\le \\frac{1}{2} \\tau^2 e^{-\\xi_{m-1}\\tau} (\\xi_m - \\xi_{m-1})^2 = \\frac{1}{2} (\\xi_{m-1}\\tau)^2 e^{-\\xi_{m-1}\\tau} (r - 1)^2 \\le \\frac{2}{e^2} (r - 1)^2$. Summing over $m$ and integrating in $L^2([0,T])$ gives the $O((r-1)^2)$ bound. $\\blacksquare$\n\n---\n\n### 4.2 Fractional Adams-Bashforth-Moulton (Diethelm) Predictor-Corrector Scheme\n\nTo solve the Fractional Riccati-Volterra Equation of Theorem 2.1.1:\n$$\\psi(t) = \\frac{1}{\\Gamma(\\alpha)} \\int_0^t (t - s)^{\\alpha - 1} F(u, \\psi(s)) \\, ds, \\quad t \\in [0,T],$$\non a uniform grid $t_n = n h$ ($n = 0, \\dots, N$, $h = T / N$), we derive the **Fractional Adams-Bashforth-Moulton Predictor-Corrector Scheme** of Diethelm, Ford, and Freed (2002).\n\n#### Proposition 4.2.1 (Exact Fractional Adams-Diethelm Quadrature Weights and Quadratic Solver)\n1. **Rectangle Predictor Weights ($O(h^\\alpha)$):** Approximating $F(u, \\psi(s))$ by a piecewise constant function on $[t_j, t_{j+1}]$ yields the explicit predictor $\\psi_{n+1}^P$:\n   $$\\psi_{n+1}^P = \\sum_{j=0}^n b_{j, n+1} F(u, \\psi_j), \\quad \\text{where } b_{j, n+1} = \\frac{h^\\alpha}{\\Gamma(\\alpha + 1)} \\left[ (n + 1 - j)^\\alpha - (n - j)^\\alpha \\right].$$\n2. **Trapezoidal Corrector Weights ($O(h^{1+\\alpha})$):** Approximating $F(u, \\psi(s))$ by a continuous piecewise linear interpolant on $[t_j, t_{j+1}]$ yields the trapezoidal rule $\\int_0^{t_{n+1}} (t_{n+1}-s)^{\\alpha-1} g(s) \\, ds \\approx \\sum_{j=0}^{n+1} a_{j, n+1} g(t_j)$, where:\n   $$a_{j, n+1} = \\frac{h^\\alpha}{\\Gamma(\\alpha + 2)} \\begin{cases} n^{\\alpha + 1} - (n - \\alpha)(n + 1)^\\alpha, & j = 0, \\\\ (n - j + 2)^{\\alpha + 1} + (n - j)^{\\alpha + 1} - 2(n - j + 1)^{\\alpha + 1}, & 1 \\le j \\le n, \\\\ 1, & j = n + 1. \\end{cases}$$\n3. **One-Step Predictor-Corrector vs. Exact Quadratic Root:** The standard Diethelm corrector is:\n   $$\\psi_{n+1} = \\sum_{j=0}^n a_{j, n+1} F(u, \\psi_j) + a_{n+1, n+1} F(u, \\psi_{n+1}^P).$$\n   Alternatively, because $F(u, \\psi) = c_0(u) + c_1(u) \\psi + c_2 \\psi^2$ is a quadratic polynomial (with $c_0(u) = \\frac{1}{2}(u^2-u)$, $c_1(u) = u\\rho\\xi - \\kappa$, $c_2 = \\frac{1}{2}\\xi^2$), the implicit corrector equation $\\psi_{n+1} = H_n + a_{n+1, n+1} (c_0 + c_1 \\psi_{n+1} + c_2 \\psi_{n+1}^2)$ where $H_n = \\sum_{j=0}^n a_{j, n+1} F(u, \\psi_j)$ can be solved **analytically** via the principal branch of the quadratic formula:\n   $$\\psi_{n+1} = \\frac{1 - a_0 c_1 - \\sqrt{(1 - a_0 c_1)^2 - 4 a_0 c_2 (H_n + a_0 c_0)}}{2 a_0 c_2}, \\quad a_0 := a_{n+1, n+1} = \\frac{h^\\alpha}{\\Gamma(\\alpha + 2)},$$\n   which is unconditionally A-stable for stiff parameters $\\kappa, \\xi \\gg 1$!\n\n##### Proof\nTo derive $a_{j, n+1}$, write the piecewise linear interpolant of $g(s)$ on $[0, t_{n+1}]$ in terms of the hat basis functions $\\phi_{j, n+1}(s)$: $g_h(s) = \\sum_{j=0}^{n+1} g(t_j) \\phi_{j, n+1}(s)$. Then $a_{j, n+1} = \\frac{1}{\\Gamma(\\alpha)} \\int_0^{t_{n+1}} (t_{n+1} - s)^{\\alpha - 1} \\phi_{j, n+1}(s) \\, ds$.\nFor $1 \\le j \\le n$, $\\phi_{j, n+1}(s) = \\frac{s - t_{j-1}}{h} \\mathbf{1}_{[t_{j-1}, t_j]}(s) + \\frac{t_{j+1} - s}{h} \\mathbf{1}_{[t_j, t_{j+1}]}(s)$. Substituting $s = t_{n+1} - h v$:\n$$\\begin{aligned}\na_{j, n+1} &= \\frac{h^\\alpha}{\\Gamma(\\alpha)} \\left[ \\int_{n+1-j}^{n+2-j} v^{\\alpha - 1} (n + 2 - j - v) \\, dv + \\int_{n-j}^{n+1-j} v^{\\alpha - 1} (v - (n - j)) \\, dv \\right] \\\\\n&= \\frac{h^\\alpha}{\\Gamma(\\alpha + 2)} \\left[ (n - j + 2)^{\\alpha + 1} + (n - j)^{\\alpha + 1} - 2(n - j + 1)^{\\alpha + 1} \\right].\n\\end{aligned}$$\nThe cases $j = 0$ and $j = n + 1$ follow identically by integrating over $[0, t_1]$ and $[t_n, t_{n+1}]$. $\\blacksquare$\n\n---\n\n### 4.3 Hybrid Scheme and Exact Cholesky Simulation for Rough Bergomi\n\nFor the Rough Bergomi model, we implement two complementary simulation methods:\n1. **Exact Covariance Cholesky Simulation:** Using Proposition 2.2.1, assemble the exact $2N \\times 2N$ covariance matrix $\\mathbf{\\Sigma}$ of the joint Gaussian vector $(\\hat{W}_{t_1}, \\dots, \\hat{W}_{t_N}, \\Delta W_1^{(1)}, \\dots, \\Delta W_N^{(1)})^\\top$, compute its lower-triangular Cholesky factor $\\mathbf{L}$ ($\\mathbf{L}\\mathbf{L}^\\top = \\mathbf{\\Sigma}$) once offline, and generate exact sample paths via matrix-vector multiplication $\\mathbf{L} \\mathbf{Z}$ with $\\mathbf{Z} \\sim \\mathcal{N}(\\mathbf{0}, \\mathbf{I}_{2N})$.\n2. **The Bennedsen-Lunde-Pakkanen (2017) Hybrid Scheme ($\\kappa_{\\text{hyb}} = 1$):** Near the singularity $s \\approx t_i$, the power-law kernel $(t_i - s)^{H - 1/2}$ cannot be accurately approximated by Riemann sums, whereas away from the diagonal it is smooth. For $\\kappa_{\\text{hyb}} = 1$, we split the integral at $t_{i-1}$:\n   $$\\hat{W}_{t_i} = \\sqrt{2H} \\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_i} (t_i - s)^{H - 1/2} \\, dW_s^{(1)} + \\sqrt{2H} \\sum_{k=1}^{i-1} b_k^* h^{H - 1/2} \\left( W_{t_{i-k}}^{(1)} - W_{t_{i-k-1}}^{(1)} \\right),$$\n   where the first term $I_{i,1} := \\sqrt{2H} \\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_i} (t_i - s)^{H - 1/2} \\, dW_s^{(1)}$ is simulated **exactly** jointly with $\\Delta W_i^{(1)} := W_{t_i}^{(1)} - W_{t_{i-1}}^{(1)}$ as a bivariate Gaussian vector with covariance matrix:\n   $$\\mathbf{\\Sigma}_{\\text{loc}} = \\begin{pmatrix} \\text{Var}(\\Delta W_i^{(1)}) & \\text{Cov}(\\Delta W_i^{(1)}, I_{i,1}) \\\\ \\text{Cov}(\\Delta W_i^{(1)}, I_{i,1}) & \\text{Var}(I_{i,1}) \\end{pmatrix} = \\begin{pmatrix} h & \\frac{\\sqrt{2H}}{H + 1/2} h^{H + 1/2} \\\\ \\frac{\\sqrt{2H}}{H + 1/2} h^{H + 1/2} & h^{2H} \\end{pmatrix},$$\n   and the optimal mean-square error weights $b_k^*$ for $k \\ge 2$ are given by:\n   $$b_k^* = \\left( \\frac{k^{H + 1/2} - (k - 1)^{H + 1/2}}{H + 1/2} \\right)^{\\frac{1}{H - 1/2}},$$\n   and the discrete convolution $\\sum_{k=2}^i b_k^* \\Delta W_{i+1-k}^{(1)}$ is evaluated in $O(N \\log N)$ operations via Fast Fourier Transform (FFT).\n\n---\n\n### 4.4 Lewis / Carr-Madan Fourier Inversion and Longstaff-Schwartz American Pricing\n\n#### Proposition 4.4.1 (Lewis (2001) Fundamental Transform Formula for European Options)\nGiven the characteristic function $\\phi_T(u) = \\mathbb{E}[e^{u X_T}]$ of $X_T = \\ln(S_T / S_0)$ under $\\mathbb{Q}$ (with drift $(r - q)T$ included in $X_T$), the price of a European call option with strike $K$ and log-moneyness $k = \\ln(K / S_0) - (r - q)T$ is given by the rapidly decaying single-integral **Lewis Formula** along the contour $\\text{Im}(z) = -1/2$:\n$$C(S_0, K, T) = S_0 e^{-q T} - \\frac{\\sqrt{S_0 K} e^{-\\frac{1}{2}(r + q)T}}{\\pi} \\int_0^\\infty \\text{Re}\\left[ e^{i v k} \\phi_T\\left( i v + \\frac{1}{2} \\right) \\right] \\frac{dv}{v^2 + \\frac{1}{4}}.$$\nBecause $|\\phi_T(iv + 1/2)| \\le \\phi_T(1/2) \\le 1$ and $(v^2 + 1/4)^{-1}$ decays quadratically, Gauss-Laguerre or adaptive Gauss-Kronrod quadrature on $[0, v_{\\max}]$ achieves machine precision in 32–64 nodes without requiring any damping tuning parameter!\n\n#### 4.4.2 Non-Markovian Longstaff-Schwartz Least-Squares Monte Carlo (LSM) for American Options\nUnder rough volatility, the asset price $S_t$ and variance $V_t$ alone do *not* form a Markov process because future variance evolution depends on the entire history of the forward variance curve $\\xi_t(u) = \\mathbb{E}[V_u \\,|\\, \\mathcal{F}_t]$ for $u \\ge t$. Following Bayer, Tempone, and Wolf (2020), the continuation value of an American option at exercise date $t_n$ is a functional of $(S_{t_n}, V_{t_n}, (\\xi_{t_n}(u))_{u \\ge t_n})$ (or equivalently the Markovian lift factors $(S_{t","type":"text"},"event_type":"step.delta"}

At $t_N = T$, set $\\text{Cashflow}_N^{(m)} = g(S_{t_N}^{(m)})$ for path $m = 1, \\dots, M_{\\text{paths}}$.\n2. For $n = N - 1, \\dots, 1$:\n   * Identify in-the-money paths $\\mathcal{I}_n = \\{ m : g(S_{t_n}^{(m)}) > 0 \\}$.\n   * Regress discounted future cashflows $Y_n^{(m)} = e^{-r(t_{\\tau_m} - t_n)} \\text{Cashflow}_{\\tau_m}^{(m)}$ for $m \\in \\mathcal{I}_n$ onto the feature matrix $\\mathbf{\\Psi}(\\mathbf{Z}_{t_n}^{(m)})$ via Ridge regression:\n     $$\\hat{\\boldsymbol{\\beta}}_n = \\left( \\mathbf{\\Psi}_n^\\top \\mathbf{\\Psi}_n + \\lambda_{\\text{ridge}} \\mathbf{I} \\right)^{-1} \\mathbf{\\Psi}_n^\\top \\mathbf{Y}_n.$$\n   * Update exercise stopping time $\\tau_m \\leftarrow n$ and $\\text{Cashflow}_n^{(m)} \\leftarrow g(S_{t_n}^{(m)})$ whenever $g(S_{t_n}^{(m)}) \\ge \\hat{C}_n(\\mathbf{Z}_{t_n}^{(m)}) = \\mathbf{\\Psi}(\\mathbf{Z}_{t_n}^{(m)}) \\hat{\\boldsymbol{\\beta}}_n$.\n\n---\n\n## Part V: Complete Production-Grade Python Quantitative Library\n\nThe complete, self-contained, fully vectorised Python library implementing every model, numerical scheme, pricing engine, Malliavin Greek estimator, Hawkes process simulator, HJB PIDE solver, and calibration routine derived in Parts I–IV is presented below.\n\n```python\n#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"\n================================================================================\nROUGH VOLATILITY & HAWKES HJB OPTIMAL MARKET-MAKING QUANTITATIVE LIBRARY\n================================================================================\nA complete, self-contained mathematical finance library implementing:\n  1. Fractional Kernel & Multi-Exponential Markovian Lift Approximations\n  2. Rough Heston Fractional Adams-Bashforth-Moulton (Diethelm) Riccati Solver\n  3. Rough Heston & Multi-Factor Rough Bergomi Exact Cholesky, Hybrid, and\n     Markovian Lift Monte Carlo Engines (with Romano-Touzi & Antithetic VR)\n  4. Lewis & Carr-Madan FFT Fourier Pricing + Short-Time Asymptotic Skew Formula\n  5. Path-Dependent Exotic Pricer (Asian, Barrier, Lookback) with Exact\n     Malliavin Integration-by-Parts (IBP) Bismut-Elworthy-Li Greeks\n     (Delta, Gamma, Vega) and Non-Markovian Longstaff-Schwartz American Pricer\n  6. Bivariate Mutually Exciting Hawkes Process Exact Ogata Thinning Simulator\n     with Spectral Stability Analysis\n  7. Coupled HJB PIDE Solver for Optimal Market Making & Exotic Hedging under\n     Rough Volatility and Hawkes Order Flow (IMEX PIDE + Riccati Asymptotics)\n  8. Joint Calibration of Rough Volatility to Implied Volatility Smiles and\n     MLE Calibration of Bivariate Hawkes Processes\n================================================================================\n\"\"\"\n\nimport math\nimport time\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\nfrom typing import Callable, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple, Union\n\nimport numpy as np\nfrom scipy import integrate, linalg, optimize, special, stats\n\n\n# ==============================================================================\n# MODULE 1: BLACK-SCHOLES UTILITIES & IMPLIED VOLATILITY SOLVER\n# ==============================================================================\n\nclass BlackScholes:\n    \"\"\"Analytical Black-Scholes formulas and Newton-Raphson/Brent implied vol solver.\"\"\"\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def call_price(\n        S: Union[float, np.ndarray],\n        K: Union[float, np.ndarray],\n        T: Union[float, np.ndarray],\n        r: float,\n        q: float,\n        sigma: Union[float, np.ndarray]\n    ) -> Union[float, np.ndarray]:\n        \"\"\"Compute Black-Scholes European call option price.\"\"\"\n        S_arr = np.asarray(S, dtype=np.float64)\n        K_arr = np.asarray(K, dtype=np.float64)\n        T_arr = np.maximum(np.asarray(T, dtype=np.float64), 1e-12)\n        sig_arr = np.maximum(np.asarray(sigma, dtype=np.float64), 1e-12)\n\n        sqrt_T = np.sqrt(T_arr)\n        d1 = (np.log(S_arr / K_arr) + (r - q + 0.5 * sig_arr ** 2) * T_arr) / (sig_arr * sqrt_T)\n        d2 = d1 - sig_arr * sqrt_T\n        price = S_arr * np.exp(-q * T_arr) * stats.norm.cdf(d1) - K_arr * np.exp(-r * T_arr) * stats.norm.cdf(d2)\n        return float(price) if np.ndim(S) == 0 and np.ndim(K) == 0 and np.ndim(sigma) == 0 else price\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def put_price(\n        S: Union[float, np.ndarray],\n        K: Union[float, np.ndarray],\n        T: Union[float, np.ndarray],\n        r: float,\n        q: float,\n        sigma: Union[float, np.ndarray]\n    ) -> Union[float, np.ndarray]:\n        \"\"\"Compute Black-Scholes European put option price via Put-Call Parity.\"\"\"\n        call = BlackScholes.call_price(S, K, T, r, q, sigma)\n        parity = call - S * np.exp(-q * T) + K * np.exp(-r * T)\n        return float(parity) if np.ndim(parity) == 0 else parity\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def vega(\n        S: float, K: float, T: float, r: float, q: float, sigma: float\n    ) -> float:\n        \"\"\"Compute Black-Scholes Vega dC/dsigma.\"\"\"\n        if T <= 1e-12 or sigma <= 1e-12:\n            return 0.0\n        sqrt_T = math.sqrt(T)\n        d1 = (math.log(S / K) + (r - q + 0.5 * sigma ** 2) * T) / (sigma * sqrt_T)\n        return float(S * math.exp(-q * T) * stats.norm.pdf(d1) * sqrt_T)\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def implied_volatility(\n        price: float,\n        S: float,\n        K: float,\n        T: float,\n        r: float = 0.0,\n        q: float = 0.0,\n        is_call: bool = True,\n        tol: float = 1e-10\n    ) -> float:\n        \"\"\"Compute Black-Scholes implied volatility using safeguarded Brent + Newton.\"\"\"\n        if not is_call:\n            price = price + S * math.exp(-q * T) - K * math.exp(-r * T)\n        intrinsic = max(0.0, S * math.exp(-q * T) - K * math.exp(-r * T))\n        upper_bound = S * math.exp(-q * T)\n        if price <= intrinsic + 1e-12:\n            return 1e-6\n        if price >= upper_bound - 1e-12:\n            return 5.0\n\n        def obj(sig: float) -> float:\n            return float(BlackScholes.call_price(S, K, T, r, q, sig)) - price\n\n        try:\n            return float(optimize.brentq(obj, 1e-6, 5.0, xtol=tol, maxiter=200))\n        except ValueError:\n            return 0.2\n\n\n# ==============================================================================\n# MODULE 2: FRACTIONAL CALCULUS & MARKOVIAN KERNEL APPROXIMATION\n# ==============================================================================\n\n@dataclass\nclass MarkovianKernelLift:\n    \"\"\"\n    Multi-exponential Markovian approximation of the Riemann-Liouville fractional kernel:\n        K_H(tau) = tau^(H - 1/2) / Gamma(H + 1/2)  ~=  sum_{m=1}^M w_m * exp(-gamma_m * tau).\n    Implements Proposition 4.1.1 (Abi Jaber & El Euch geometric partition quadrature).\n    \"\"\"\n    H: float\n    M: int = 16\n    gamma_min: float = 1e-3\n    gamma_max: float = 5e3\n    weights: np.ndarray = field(init=False)\n    nodes: np.ndarray = field(init=False)\n\n    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n        if not (0.0 < self.H < 0.5):\n            raise ValueError(f\"Hurst parameter H must be in (0, 0.5), got {self.H}.\")\n        alpha = self.H + 0.5\n        # Geometric partition of Laplace frequency domain [gamma_min, gamma_max]\n        xi = np.geomspace(self.gamma_min, self.gamma_max, self.M + 1)\n        c_measure = 1.0 / (special.gamma(alpha) * special.gamma(1.0 - alpha))\n\n        p1 = 0.5 - self.H\n        p2 = 1.5 - self.H\n        self.weights = (c_measure / p1) * (xi[1:] ** p1 - xi[:-1] ** p1)\n        self.nodes = (p1 / p2) * (xi[1:] ** p2 - xi[:-1] ** p2) / (xi[1:] ** p1 - xi[:-1] ** p1)\n\n        # Least-squares fine-tuning of weights on a log-spaced time grid in [1e-4, 2.0]\n        tau_grid = np.geomspace(1e-4, 2.0, 200)\n        exact_k = self.exact_kernel(tau_grid)\n        exp_mat = np.exp(- np.outer(tau_grid, self.nodes))\n        # Non-negative least squares refinement to improve practical L2 accuracy\n        w_opt, _ = optimize.nnls(exp_mat * np.sqrt(tau_grid[:, None]), exact_k * np.sqrt(tau_grid))\n        if np.sum(w_opt) > 0:\n            self.weights = 0.5 * self.weights + 0.5 * w_opt\n\n    def exact_kernel(self, tau: Union[float, np.ndarray]) -> Union[float, np.ndarray]:\n        \"\"\"Evaluate exact Riemann-Liouville kernel tau^(H - 1/2) / Gamma(H + 1/2).\"\"\"\n        tau_arr = np.asarray(tau, dtype=np.float64)\n        return np.power(np.maximum(tau_arr, 1e-15), self.H - 0.5) / special.gamma(self.H + 0.5)\n\n    def approx_kernel(self, tau: Union[float, np.ndarray]) -> Union[float, np.ndarray]:\n        \"\"\"Evaluate multi-exponential approximated kernel sum_{m=1}^M w_m exp(-gamma_m * tau).\"\"\"\n        tau_arr = np.asarray(tau, dtype=np.float64)\n        if tau_arr.ndim == 0:\n            return float(np.sum(self.weights * np.exp(-self.nodes * float(tau_arr))))\n        return np.exp(-np.outer(tau_arr, self.nodes)) @ self.weights\n\n    def l2_error(self, T: float = 1.0, n_quad: int = 500) -> float:\n        \"\"\"Compute numerical L^2([0, T]) error ||K_H - K_H^(M)||_{L^2([0,T])}.\"\"\"\n        # Use Gauss-Jacobi or dense power-law grid to resolve singularity at tau = 0\n        u = np.linspace(1e-6, 1.0, n_quad)\n        tau = T * (u ** (1.0 / (2.0 * self.H)))\n        dtau = np.gradient(tau)\n        diff_sq = (self.exact_kernel(tau) - self.approx_kernel(tau)) ** 2\n        return float(np.sqrt(np.sum(diff_sq * dtau)))\n\n\n# ==============================================================================\n# MODULE 3: ROUGH HESTON MODEL (FRACTIONAL RICCATI, FOURIER PRICER, MONTE CARLO)\n# ==============================================================================\n\n@dataclass\nclass RoughHestonParams:\n    \"\"\"Parameters of the Rough Heston fractional stochastic volatility model.\"\"\"\n    H: float = 0.10         # Hurst parameter in (0, 0.5)\n    kappa: float = 1.50     # Mean-reversion speed\n    theta: float = 0.04     # Long-run variance level\n    xi: float = 0.35        # Vol-of-vol\n    rho: float = -0.70      # Spot-vol correlation\n    v0: float = 0.04        # Initial instantaneous variance V_0\n\n    @property\n    def alpha(self) -> float:\n        return self.H + 0.5\n\n\nclass RoughHestonEngine:\n    \"\"\"\n    Comprehensive Rough Heston engine implementing:\n      1. Fractional Adams-Bashforth-Moulton (Diethelm) Riccati-Volterra solver\n         with exact implicit quadratic root (Proposition 4.2.1 & Theorem 2.1.1)\n      2. Characteristic function evaluation\n      3. Lewis (2001) & Carr-Madan (1999) Fourier European option pricing\n      4. Multi-factor Markovian lift & Volterra Euler Monte Carlo simulation\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, params: RoughHestonParams, n_riccati_steps: int = 250):\n        self.params = params\n        self.n_riccati_steps = n_riccati_steps\n\n    def riccati_rhs(\n        self, u: Union[complex, np.ndarray], psi: Union[complex, np.ndarray]\n    ) -> Union[complex, np.ndarray]:\n        \"\"\"Evaluate Riccati nonlinearity F(u, psi) = 0.5*(u^2 - u) + (u*rho*xi - kappa)*psi + 0.5*xi^2*psi^2.\"\"\"\n        p = self.params\n        return 0.5 * (u * u - u) + (u * p.rho * p.xi - p.kappa) * psi + 0.5 * (p.xi ** 2) * (psi * psi)\n\n    def solve_fractional_riccati(\n        self, T: float, u_vec: np.ndarray, n_steps: Optional[int] = None\n    ) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n        \"\"\"\n        Vectorized Fractional Adams-Bashforth-Moulton Predictor-Corrector solver\n        with exact quadratic root for the Fractional Riccati-Volterra equation:\n            D^alpha psi(t, u) = F(u, psi(t, u)),   I^(1-alpha) psi(0, u) = 0.\n        Solves simultaneously for an entire vector of complex frequencies u_vec.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        t_grid : np.ndarray of shape (N + 1,)\n        psi_grid : np.ndarray of shape (N + 1, len(u_vec))\n        F_grid : np.ndarray of shape (N + 1, len(u_vec))\n        \"\"\"\n        N = n_steps if n_steps is not None else self.n_riccati_steps\n        alpha = self.params.alpha\n        p = self.params\n        h = T / N\n        t_grid = np.linspace(0.0, T, N + 1)\n\n        u_arr = np.atleast_1d(np.asarray(u_vec, dtype=np.complex128))\n        M_u = len(u_arr)\n\n        psi_grid = np.zeros((N + 1, M_u), dtype=np.complex128)\n        F_grid = np.zeros((N + 1, M_u), dtype=np.complex128)\n        F_grid[0, :] = 0.5 * (u_arr ** 2 - u_arr)\n\n        # Precompute Diethelm predictor and corrector convolution weights\n        k_idx = np.arange(0, N + 2, dtype=np.float64)\n        h_alpha_pred = (h ** alpha) / special.gamma(alpha + 1.0)\n        b_weights = h_alpha_pred * ((k_idx[1:] ** alpha) - (k_idx[:-1] ** alpha))\n\n        h_alpha_corr = (h ** alpha) / special.gamma(alpha + 2.0)\n        a_conv = np.zeros(N + 1, dtype=np.float64)\n        if N >= 1:\n            j_diff = np.arange(1, N + 1, dtype=np.float64)\n            a_conv[1:] = h_alpha_corr * (\n                (j_diff + 1.0) ** (alpha + 1.0)\n                + (j_diff - 1.0) ** (alpha + 1.0)\n                - 2.0 * (j_diff ** (alpha + 1.0))\n            )\n        a0_self = h_alpha_corr  # Weight a_{n+1, n+1}\n\n        c0 = 0.5 * (u_arr ** 2 - u_arr)\n        c1 = u_arr * p.rho * p.xi - p.kappa\n        c2 = 0.5 * (p.xi ** 2)\n\n        for n in range(N):\n            # 1. History integral H_n = sum_{j=0}^n a_{j, n+1} F(u, psi_j)\n            a_0_np1 = h_alpha_corr * ((n ** (alpha + 1.0)) - (n - alpha) * ((n + 1.0) ** alpha))\n            H_n = a_0_np1 * F_grid[0, :]\n            if n >= 1:\n                # For 1 <= j <= n, weight depends on n - j + 1 = d in {n, ..., 1}\n                weights_mid = a_conv[n:0:-1]\n                H_n = H_n + weights_mid @ F_grid[1:n + 1, :]\n\n            # 2. Predictor psi_{n+1}^P = sum_{j=0}^n b_{n-j} F_j\n            b_slice = b_weights[n::-1]\n            psi_pred = b_slice @ F_grid[:n + 1, :]\n            F_pred = c0 + c1 * psi_pred + c2 * (psi_pred ** 2)\n            psi_corr_one_step = H_n + a0_self * F_pred\n\n            # 3. Exact implicit quadratic root polishing:\n            #    a0 * c2 * psi^2 + (a0 * c1 - 1) * psi + (H_n + a0 * c0) = 0\n            B_quad = 1.0 - a0_self * c1\n            disc = B_quad * B_quad - 4.0 * a0_self * c2 * (H_n + a0_self * c0)\n            sqrt_disc = np.sqrt(disc)\n            # Choose principal root closest to the Adams-Moulton predictor-corrector step\n            root1 = (B_quad - sqrt_disc) / (2.0 * a0_self * c2)\n            root2 = (B_quad + sqrt_disc) / (2.0 * a0_self * c2)\n            use_root1 = np.abs(root1 - psi_corr_one_step) <= np.abs(root2 - psi_corr_one_step)\n            psi_next = np.where(use_root1, root1, root2)\n\n            psi_grid[n + 1, :] = psi_next\n            F_grid[n + 1, :] = c0 + c1 * psi_next + c2 * (psi_next ** 2)\n\n        return t_grid, psi_grid, F_grid\n\n    def characteristic_function(\n        self,\n","type":"text"},"event_type":"step.delta"}

{"index":1,"delta":{"text":"        u_vec: Union[complex, np.ndarray],\n        T: float,\n        r: float = 0.0,\n        q: float = 0.0,\n        n_steps: Optional[int] = None\n    ) -> np.ndarray:\n        \"\"\"\n        Compute the Rough Heston characteristic function E[exp(u * X_T)]\n        using Theorem 2.1.1:\n            phi_T(u) = exp( u*(r - q)*T + kappa*theta * int_0^T psi(s, u) ds + V_0 * int_0^T F(u, psi(s, u)) ds ).\n        \"\"\"\n        scalar_input = np.ndim(u_vec) == 0\n        u_arr = np.atleast_1d(np.asarray(u_vec, dtype=np.complex128))\n        t_grid, psi_grid, F_grid = self.solve_fractional_riccati(T, u_arr, n_steps=n_steps)\n\n        # Trapezoidal integration of psi(s, u) and F(u, psi(s, u)) over [0, T]\n        int_psi = integrate.trapezoid(psi_grid, t_grid, axis=0)\n        int_F = integrate.trapezoid(F_grid, t_grid, axis=0)\n\n        p = self.params\n        exponent = u_arr * (r - q) * T + p.kappa * p.theta * int_psi + p.v0 * int_F\n        cf = np.exp(exponent)\n        return cf[0] if scalar_input else cf\n\n    def price_european_lewis(\n        self,\n        S0: float,\n        K_vec: Union[float, np.ndarray],\n        T: float,\n        r: float = 0.0,\n        q: float = 0.0,\n        is_call: bool = True,\n        n_quad: int = 64,\n        v_max: float = 120.0\n    ) -> Union[float, np.ndarray]:\n        \"\"\"\n        Price European Call/Put options via the Lewis (2001) contour integral formula\n        (Proposition 4.4.1) using Gauss-Legendre quadrature on [0, v_max].\n        \"\"\"\n        scalar_input = np.ndim(K_vec) == 0\n        K_arr = np.atleast_1d(np.asarray(K_vec, dtype=np.float64))\n\n        # Gauss-Legendre nodes on [0, v_max] with hyperbolic stretching for rapid convergence\n        nodes_std, weights_std = special.roots_legendre(n_quad)\n        # Map [-1, 1] -> [0, 1] -> [0, v_max] via quadratic clustering near 0\n        u_01 = 0.5 * (nodes_std + 1.0)\n        v_nodes = v_max * (u_01 ** 2)\n        dv_weights = 0.5 * weights_std * (2.0 * v_max * u_01)\n\n        # Evaluate characteristic function at u = i*v + 0.5 (with drift 0, handled in k)\n        u_complex = 1j * v_nodes + 0.5\n        cf_vals = self.characteristic_function(u_complex, T, r=0.0, q=0.0)\n\n        # Log-moneyness relative to forward price F = S0 * exp((r - q) * T)\n        F0 = S0 * math.exp((r - q) * T)\n        k_arr = np.log(K_arr / F0)  # shape (M_K,)\n\n        # Integrand Re[ exp(i * v * k) * cf(i*v + 0.5) ] / (v^2 + 0.25)\n        exp_ivk = np.exp(1j * np.outer(k_arr, v_nodes))  # shape (M_K, n_quad)\n        integrand = np.real(exp_ivk * cf_vals[None, :]) / (v_nodes[None, :] ** 2 + 0.25)\n        integral_vals = integrand @ dv_weights\n\n        call_prices = S0 * math.exp(-q * T) - (\n            np.sqrt(S0 * K_arr) * math.exp(-0.5 * (r + q) * T) / math.pi\n        ) * integral_vals\n        call_prices = np.maximum(call_prices, np.maximum(0.0, S0 * math.exp(-q * T) - K_arr * math.exp(-r * T)))\n\n        if is_call:\n            return float(call_prices[0]) if scalar_input else call_prices\n        put_prices = call_prices - S0 * math.exp(-q * T) + K_arr * math.exp(-r * T)\n        return float(put_prices[0]) if scalar_input else put_prices\n\n    def price_european_carr_madan_fft(\n        self,\n        S0: float,\n        K_vec: np.ndarray,\n        T: float,\n        r: float = 0.0,\n        q: float = 0.0,\n        alpha_damp: float = 1.25,\n        N_fft: int = 512,\n        eta_step: float = 0.15\n    ) -> np.ndarray:\n        \"\"\"\n        Price European Call options across a strike grid using the Carr-Madan (1999)\n        damped Fast Fourier Transform method with Simpson's rule weights.\n        \"\"\"\n        v_j = np.arange(N_fft, dtype=np.float64) * eta_step\n        lambda_k = 2.0 * math.pi / (N_fft * eta_step)\n        b_bound = 0.5 * N_fft * lambda_k\n        k_grid = -b_bound + np.arange(N_fft, dtype=np.float64) * lambda_k  # log(K / S0)\n\n        # Evaluate characteristic function of log(S_T / S0) at u = i * (v_j - (alpha + 1) * i) = (alpha + 1) + i * v_j\n        u_eval = (alpha_damp + 1.0) + 1j * v_j\n        cf_vals = self.characteristic_function(u_eval, T, r=r, q=q)\n\n        denom = (alpha_damp ** 2 + alpha_damp - v_j ** 2) + 1j * (2.0 * alpha_damp + 1.0) * v_j\n        psi_cm = math.exp(-r * T) * cf_vals / denom\n\n        # Simpson's 1/3 rule weights\n        simpson = np.ones(N_fft, dtype=np.float64)\n        simpson[1:-1:2] = 4.0\n        simpson[2:-2:2] = 2.0\n        simpson /= 3.0\n\n        fft_input = np.exp(1j * b_bound * v_j) * psi_cm * eta_step * simpson\n        fft_output = np.fft.fft(fft_input)\n        call_grid = S0 * (np.exp(-alpha_damp * k_grid) / math.pi) * np.real(fft_output)\n\n        log_strikes_target = np.log(np.asarray(K_vec, dtype=np.float64) / S0)\n        return np.interp(log_strikes_target, k_grid, call_grid)\n\n    def simulate_paths_markovian_lift(\n        self,\n        S0: float,\n        T: float,\n        n_steps: int = 100,\n        n_paths: int = 10000,\n        r: float = 0.0,\n        q: float = 0.0,\n        M_factors: int = 12,\n        seed: int = 42\n    ) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n        \"\"\"\n        Simulate Rough Heston sample paths using the M-factor Markovian Lift\n        (Section 3.2.1 & 4.1) with full truncation reflection for variance positivity\n        and antithetic variates.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        t_grid : (n_steps + 1,)\n        S_paths : (n_paths, n_steps + 1)\n        V_paths : (n_paths, n_steps + 1)\n        \"\"\"\n        rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n        p = self.params\n        lift = MarkovianKernelLift(H=p.H, M=M_factors)\n        w_m = lift.weights      # shape (M,)\n        gamma_m = lift.nodes    # shape (M,)\n\n        dt = T / n_steps\n        sqrt_dt = math.sqrt(dt)\n        t_grid = np.linspace(0.0, T, n_steps + 1)\n\n        half_paths = n_paths // 2\n        S_paths = np.zeros((n_paths, n_steps + 1), dtype=np.float64)\n        V_paths = np.zeros((n_paths, n_steps + 1), dtype=np.float64)\n        S_paths[:, 0] = S0\n        V_paths[:, 0] = p.v0\n\n        # Markovian state factors Y^{(m)}_t of shape (n_paths, M)\n        Y_state = np.zeros((n_paths, M_factors), dtype=np.float64)\n        exp_decay = np.exp(-gamma_m * dt)  # Exact exponential integrator for -gamma_m * Y^{(m)}\n        int_decay = (1.0 - exp_decay) / gamma_m\n\n        # Baseline input curve g_0(t) = v0 + kappa * theta * sum_m w_m * (1 - exp(-gamma_m * t)) / gamma_m\n        g0_grid = p.v0 + p.kappa * p.theta * (\n            (1.0 - np.exp(-np.outer(t_grid, gamma_m))) / gamma_m[None, :]\n        ) @ w_m\n\n        rho_bar = math.sqrt(1.0 - p.rho ** 2)\n\n        for i in range(n_steps):\n            z1_half = rng.standard_normal(half_paths)\n            z2_half = rng.standard_normal(half_paths)\n            z1 = np.concatenate([z1_half, -z1_half])\n            z2 = np.concatenate([z2_half, -z2_half])\n            if n_paths % 2 == 1:\n                z1 = np.append(z1, rng.standard_normal())\n                z2 = np.append(z2, rng.standard_normal())\n\n            dW1 = sqrt_dt * z1\n            dW2 = sqrt_dt * z2\n\n            V_pos = np.maximum(V_paths[:, i], 0.0)\n            sqrt_V = np.sqrt(V_pos)\n\n            # Log-Euler step for S_t\n            d_logS = (r - q - 0.5 * V_pos) * dt + sqrt_V * (p.rho * dW1 + rho_bar * dW2)\n            S_paths[:, i + 1] = S_paths[:, i] * np.exp(d_logS)\n\n            # Exponential-Euler step for each Markovian factor Y^{(m)}\n            # dY^{(m)} = (-gamma_m Y^{(m)} - kappa V_t) dt + xi sqrt(V_t) dW_t^{(1)}\n            Y_state = (\n                Y_state * exp_decay[None, :]\n                - p.kappa * V_pos[:, None] * int_decay[None, :]\n                + p.xi * (sqrt_V * dW1)[:, None] * np.exp(-0.5 * gamma_m[None, :] * dt)\n            )\n            V_next = g0_grid[i + 1] + Y_state @ w_m\n            V_paths[:, i + 1] = np.maximum(V_next, 1e-8)\n\n        return t_grid, S_paths, V_paths\n\n\n# ==============================================================================\n# MODULE 4: MULTI-FACTOR ROUGH BERGOMI MODEL & SHORT-TIME ASYMPTOTICS\n# ==============================================================================\n\n@dataclass\nclass RoughBergomiParams:\n    \"\"\"\n    Parameters for Single-Factor or Multi-Factor Rough Bergomi model.\n    Supports K rough factors with distinct Hurst exponents H_k, weights eta_k,\n    and correlations rho_k.\n    \"\"\"\n    H: Union[float, Tuple[float, ...]] = 0.10\n    eta: Union[float, Tuple[float, ...]] = 1.90\n    rho: Union[float, Tuple[float, ...]] = -0.75\n    xi0: Union[float, Callable[[np.ndarray], np.ndarray]] = 0.04\n\n    def get_xi0(self, t: Union[float, np.ndarray]) -> np.ndarray:\n        t_arr = np.asarray(t, dtype=np.float64)\n        if callable(self.xi0):\n            return np.asarray(self.xi0(t_arr), dtype=np.float64)\n        return np.full_like(t_arr, float(self.xi0), dtype=np.float64)\n\n\nclass RoughBergomiEngine:\n    \"\"\"\n    Rough Bergomi simulation and asymptotics engine implementing:\n      1. Exact Covariance Cholesky Method (Proposition 2.2.1)\n      2. Bennedsen-Lunde-Pakkanen (2017) FFT Hybrid Scheme (Section 4.3)\n      3. Multi-Factor Rough Bergomi Simulation\n      4. Romano-Touzi Conditional Monte Carlo Variance Reduction (Proposition 2.2.2)\n      5. Short-Time LDP Rate Function & Exact Implied Volatility Skew Explosion\n         Formula (Theorem 2.3.1)\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, params: RoughBergomiParams):\n        self.params = params\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def g_hypergeom(x: np.ndarray, H: float) -> np.ndarray:\n        \"\"\"\n        Evaluate the exact rBergomi covariance scaling function G(x) for x >= 1\n        from Proposition 2.2.1:\n            G(x) = (2*H / (H + 0.5)) * x^(H - 0.5) * 2F1(0.5 - H, 1, H + 1.5, 1/x).\n        \"\"\"\n        x_arr = np.maximum(np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64), 1.0)\n        gamma = 0.5 - H\n        prefactor = 2.0 * H / (1.0 - gamma)\n        return prefactor * (x_arr ** (-gamma)) * special.hyp2f1(gamma, 1.0, 2.0 - gamma, 1.0 / x_arr)\n\n    def build_exact_covariance_matrix(self, t_grid: np.ndarray, H: float) -> np.ndarray:\n        \"\"\"\n        Construct the exact 2N x 2N covariance matrix Sigma for the joint Gaussian vector:\n            [ W_hat(t_1), ..., W_hat(t_N),  dW^{(1)}_1, ..., dW^{(1)}_N ]^T\n        using Proposition 2.2.1.\n        \"\"\"\n        t_pos = t_grid[1:]  # shape (N,)\n        N = len(t_pos)\n        Sigma = np.zeros((2 * N, 2 * N), dtype=np.float64)\n\n        # 1. Block (0:N, 0:N): Cov(W_hat(t_i), W_hat(t_j))\n        t_i = t_pos[:, None]\n        t_j = t_pos[None, :]\n        s_min = np.minimum(t_i, t_j)\n        t_max = np.maximum(t_i, t_j)\n        ratio = t_max / s_min\n        Sigma[:N, :N] = (s_min ** (2.0 * H)) * self.g_hypergeom(ratio, H)\n\n        # 2. Block (0:N, N:2N): Cov(W_hat(t_i), W^{(1)}(t_j) - W^{(1)}(t_{j-1}))\n        t_left = t_grid[:-1][None, :]   # u = t_{j-1}\n        t_right = t_grid[1:][None, :]   # v = t_j\n        u_cap = np.minimum(t_i, t_left)\n        v_cap = np.minimum(t_i, t_right)\n        alpha = H + 0.5\n        cov_cross = (math.sqrt(2.0 * H) / alpha) * (\n            np.maximum(t_i - u_cap, 0.0) ** alpha - np.maximum(t_i - v_cap, 0.0) ** alpha\n        )\n        Sigma[:N, N:] = cov_cross\n        Sigma[N:, :N] = cov_cross.T\n\n        # 3. Block (N:2N, N:2N): Cov(dW_i, dW_j) = dt_i * delta_{ij}\n        dt_vec = np.diff(t_grid)\n        Sigma[N:, N:] = np.diag(dt_vec)\n\n        return Sigma\n\n    def simulate_paths_cholesky(\n        self,\n        S0: float,\n        T: float,\n        n_steps: int = 100,\n        n_paths: int = 10000,\n        r: float = 0.0,\n        q: float = 0.0,\n        seed: int = 42\n    ) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, np.ndarray, np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n        \"\"\"\n        Simulate exact single-factor rBergomi paths using Cholesky decomposition\n        of the exact joint covariance matrix + antithetic variates.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        t_grid : (n_steps + 1,)\n        S_paths : (n_paths, n_steps + 1)\n        V_paths : (n_paths, n_steps + 1)\n        W_hat_paths : (n_paths, n_steps + 1)\n        dW1_paths : (n_paths, n_steps)\n        \"\"\"\n        rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n        H = float(self.params.H) if not isinstance(self.params.H, tuple) else float(self.params.H[0])\n        eta = float(self.params.eta) if not isinstance(self.params.eta, tuple) else float(self.params.eta[0])\n        rho = float(self.params.rho) if not isinstance(self.params.rho, tuple) else float(self.params.rho[0])\n\n        t_grid = np.linspace(0.0, T, n_steps + 1)\n        dt = T / n_steps\n        sqrt_dt = math.sqrt(dt)\n\n        Sigma = self.build_exact_covariance_matrix(t_grid, H)\n        #   S0: float,\n        T: float,\n        H_tuple: Tuple[float, ...] = (0.07, 0.25),\n        eta_tuple: Tuple[float, ...] = (1.6, 0.9),\n        rho_tuple: Tuple[float, ...] = (-0.65, -0.45),\n  Simulate the K-Factor Rough Bergomi model (Section 2.2) with K independent\n        or correlated fractional Volterra factors having distinct Hurst indices H_k.\n        \"\"\"\n        K_factors = sigma_eff = np.sqrt(np.maximum((1.0 - rho ** 2) * int_V / T, 1e-12))\n\n        # Vectorized Black-Scholes evaluation over (n_paths, len(K_arr))\n        if is_call:\n            bs_matrix = S_eff[:, None], K_arr[None, :], T, r, q, sigma_eff[:, None]\n            )\n        prices = np.mean(bs_matrix, axis=0)\n        return float(prices[0]) if scalar_input else prices\n\n    def asymptotic_atm_skew_and_smile(\n        self, k_vec: Union[float, np.ndarray], T: float\n    ) -> Tuple[float, np.ndarray]:\n        \"\"\"\n        Compute the exact short-time asymptotic ATM implied volatility skew\n        and first-order implied volatility smile from Theorem 2.3.1:\n            d sigma_BS / dk |_{k=0} = [ rho * eta * sqrt(2H) / (2 * (H + 0.5) * (H + 1.5)) ] * T^(H - 0.5).\n        \"\"\"\n        H = float(self.params.H) if not isinstance(self.params.H, tuple) else float(self.params.H[0])\n        eta = float(self.params.eta) if not isinstance(self.params.eta, tuple) else coefficient from short-time Malliavin/LDP expansion\n        curvature_coeff = (\n            (eta ** 2) / (4.0 * sigma0)\n            * (2.0 * H / ((H + 0.5) ** 2 * (2.0 * H + 1.0)))\n            * (1.0 + 3.0 * (rho ** 2))\n            * (T ** (2.0 * H - 1.0))\n        )\n        k_arr = np.asarray(k_vec, dtype=np.float64)\n        iv_approx = sigma0 + atm_skew * k_arr + 0.5 * curvature_coeff * ==============================================================================\n\n@dataclass\nclass BivariateHawkesParams:\n    \"\"\"\n    Parameters for a Bivariate Mutually Exciting Exponential
